# Search API (V1) conformance matrix

This document tracks how the search engine implementation conforms to the Search API contract (V1), clause by clause. Last verified against the code and issue tracker: 2026-08-08.

Status values:

- **implemented** — verified against the code; behavior matches the clause.
- **partial** — some but not all of the clause is met.
- **missing** — no implementation exists for this requirement.
- **divergent** — an implementation exists but behaves materially differently than the clause specifies.
- **n/a** — out of scope for this contract at V1 (deployment policy, or explicitly excluded).

Gap rows state what is needed to close the clause and name the open issue that tracks it (**none** when no open issue exists). Gaps spanning several clauses are grouped by root cause in "Open gaps" at the end.

| C3     | GET /schema returns contract-stable JSON Schema for request/response from GA                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | partial     | The dev-only V1 schema may change through explicitly approved breaking migrations before GA. | C148   | GET /catalogue returns `{catalogue_version, counts, examples}` from the published search catalogue, each example `{text, template, stat_var, place}`; `?place=<country dcid>` returns the counts at that country and the examples with data there, filled with its name; a place that is not a `country/` dcid or that the catalogue does not count refuses request_invalid, 400; no loaded catalogue answers service_unavailable, 503; a 200 carries `Cache-Control: public, max-age=300` | implemented | Pinned by `tests/search_catalogue/test_route.py`. The contract has no clause for this route.                                        |
| C7     | Base URLs, authentication, rate limits, CORS are deployment policy, outside contract scope                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | n/a         |                                                                                                                                     |

                                           | implemented |                                                                                                                                        |
| C11    | raw_text carries one field, query, a string of at most 2,000 characters; a query holding a lone surrogate refuses request_invalid, 400, at `/input/query`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | implemented |                                                                                                                                        |
| C12    | parsed carries parts (list of variable_text/entities/time_text/unit_text/sources/operations/open_roles/separate) plus optional raw_query, all capped and validated; a part's `sources` name or exclude the sources it answers from (C78b), each by its words or by an admitted reference, and a reference the origin catalogue does not admit refuses request_invalid with a pointer to that mention; the deprecated `provenance` and `source_text` fold into one included mention each; each part's operations are echoed onto the part it resolves. An entity of a part carries `text`, an optional `name`, an optional `role_text`, an optional pinned `dcid` (C50d) and `members`, default false. `text` is the request's words for the entity. A row's identity within the part is its `dcid`, else its `name`, else its `text` (`ParsedEntity.identity`): its role, members flag and the part's dedupe key go by it. Two rows pinning one dcid are one entity, whose words are the first row's, whatever their texts. A row no dcid pins whose identity, its `name` or else its `text`, is a dcid another row of the part pins refuses request_invalid at that row (`{part}/entities/{row}`), since the two would bind as one entity; an entity option the door would refuse that way is not offered. `name` is for a row no dcid pins, whose words could name more than one entity, so two rows written alike under two names ("Congo" from the Democratic Republic of the Congo, "Congo" to the Republic of the Congo) stay two entities and ask two choices; a row posting both `name` and `dcid` refuses with a validation error. Resolution reads `text` beside `name` as the raw_text door reads a query's words beside an extracted name (`surface_resolution.resolve_names`): "Congolese" under the name "Congo" asks the choice "Congo" asks, where "Congolese" alone resolves nothing. A refusal quotes `text`. A `name` equal to `text` is the field left out, and without it `text` is the identity. A `text` that differs from `name` only in case reads as the name alone. One part resolves an unpinned entity through one text, so a part that posts one unpinned identity under two texts that differ beyond case refuses request_invalid at `{part}/entities`. `members: true` asks for the members of the set the text names, each as its own series. The Spec binds the countries of a continent or UN subregion, or the members of a group, where it would otherwise bind the set's own node when that node holds the series. It has no effect on a single place or on a group with no node. Entity text whose wording names members reads as `members: true` without the flag: the rows of `entity_resolution/data/query_aliases.json` that declare `members` (`eu countries`, `eu member states`, `european union member states`, `dac members`). One part names a set once on each flow end its `role_text` gives, so a part may post one entity with `members` true on one end and false on the other, and reads it as its members on the first end and, on the second, as an entity without the flag reads. A part that posts the same entity with both values on one end, or with both values and a row whose `role_text` is missing or unrecognised, refuses request_invalid at `{part}/entities`. The part's `restatement` states the flag. Every request part (a parsed part and each dataset_resubmit entry) carries at most 8 operations, at least the 8 extract keeps per query (`extract.MAX_OPERATIONS`), so a part the engine echoes always posts back. A posted operation phrase is caller-authored and reaches the bind prompt as one list line, so `Operation.phrase` is capped at 200 characters, the longest phrase extract echoes, and a blank phrase or one holding a newline, a Unicode line or paragraph separator (U+2028, U+2029) or another control character refuses with a validation error. An extracted phrase must have its words in the query in order and pass the same model as written, or extract discards the operation; a phrase over 200 characters is discarded whole, since a cut phrase still reads in order and asks for less than the query did. Phrase and query are compared NFKC-normalised and casefolded, so halfwidth katakana and fullwidth Latin and digits match their ordinary forms, and each space-separated piece of a phrase is matched on its own. A piece holding a character of a script written without word spaces (CJK and its iteration mark, Hiragana, Katakana, Thai, Lao, Khmer, Myanmar, Tibetan) has no word edges, so it must occur in the query as an exact substring, in order: "上位5ドナー" in "教育援助の上位5ドナー" and "最大的五个捐助国" in "教育援助最大的五个捐助国是哪些" are admitted, "最大的十个捐助国" in the latter is not. A Latin or digit piece, and the Latin or digit end of a mixed piece, matches only where the neighbouring query character is absent, is no letter or digit, or belongs to one of those scripts: "TOP5" in "教育援助額TOP5" and "top 5" in "教育援助のtop 5ドナー" are admitted, "top 5 ドナー" in "教育援助の top 50 ドナー" and "上位5" in "教育援助の上位50ドナー" are not. A digit end also refuses a group or decimal separator followed by a digit, so "top 10" in "top 10,000 donors" and "top 2" in "top 2.5 percent" are not admitted. Hangul is written with spaces, so a Korean piece matches whole words only. `Operation.target_year`, a year from 1000 to 2200 (`MAX_FORECAST_TARGET_YEAR`), is the year a forecast projects to; it refuses on any other verb, rides with the part through every door and restatement, and does not affect resolution. The model reads no clock, so a posted value within the bound is taken as given and a stored part posts back in any later year; the raw_text door alone holds it to a year after the current one | implemented |                                                                                                                                        |
| C12a   | A parsed part's `open_roles` lists at most two role words, each a `role_text` word read through `role_axis_for_text` (`creditor`, `donor`, `recipient`, `debtor`, `origin`, `destination`, or a synonym), and each names a flow axis the part asks every member of. A word that names no role, and a word that names an axis an entity of the same part binds through `role_text`, refuse `request_invalid`, 400, at the word's pointer (`/input/parts/{i}/open_roles/{j}` on the parsed door), before any model call, on the parsed door and on a dataset_resubmit parsed entry (`entry.parsed._open_axes_for_part`). An entity the part names without `role_text` and binds on the axis at place binding wins over the open role: the open role drops and the part warns `query_qualifier_dropped` (`region.result.with_dropped_open_roles`). An open role a role-bearing answer declares no role on drops too: the answer is the total over that axis, and the part warns `query_qualifier_dropped` with a `breakdown_not_applied` departure, once per axis, so an operation over the same axis that already warned raises nothing more. With two open roles the answer serves the largest subset of them that one member the bound roles elect, or a role twin of one, declares (C69b), both when such a member declares both, and an axis left out drops and warns the same way. Where such members declare each axis but none declares both, neither axis outranks the other and both drop. Two parts that differ only in `open_roles` stay distinct. The part's restatement echoes the axes it opened                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | implemented |                                                                                                                                        |
| C13    | a dataset_resubmit identity entry edits a spec by posting its identity back with changes applied. The identity alone defines the spec: directional entities remain in `identity.entities`, and a role asked for member by member is named in `identity.open_roles`. Binding the axis in `entities` closes it. An open role the identity names is served or refuses `open_role_unavailable` (C69b) | C23    | The options door derives the identity's own StatVar population directly (engine.options.population.options_population), running no resubmit pipeline. It refuses population_empty when the identity's shape denotes no population, scope_too_broad past the graph-enumeration member cap, invalid_role_ref for a posted entity ref the graph does not carry or a flow-role entity of a kind its role does not take (search.role_kinds, the rule a dataset_resubmit identity entry and the Datasets mint refuse by), role_signature_incompatible when the population's declared roles disagree, and exact_binding_unsupported for a posted slot bound state="exact", which no Search door serves. The checks it shares with a dataset_resubmit identity entry (the pinned-ask refusal, entity typing) reuse that door's own refusal vocabulary without resolving a Spec to reach them | C134   | `kind="dataset_resubmit"` posts every part of a multi-variable result as one input; the response is the full N-part envelope, with `rendered_sentence`/`sentence_plan` composed over all N parts (C129) and same-shape parts folded exactly as a fresh resolve would (C51a). Each response part exposes `separate: true` when compared; identity, candidates and placeholder entries repost that flag to preserve it through repeated resubmissions. False is omitted. The flag stays outside Spec identities and memo keys; a memo hit applies the current entry's flag. A posted identity is read at the dcids it lists on every role. A posted continent or subregion dcid (`MembershipPolicy.holds_region`) is read at its own node, as the Datasets mint reads it, so `[africa]` for a series the `africa` node holds no observation of answers `no_data` with `no_observations` whatever its member countries hold. A place set is expanded to its members once, when a first answer resolves its name. A rebuilt definite part serves the posted group on `resolved.entity_groups` while its role keeps its entities (C172), and its spec sentence holds heading and byline tokens only, the same sentence a fresh render of that spec states; its departures ride `departures` and the composed sentence | C137   | On a `candidates` entry, `ordering` is the client's own echo and the returned `candidates` come back in posted order, always, never re-sorted -- posted order is the only record of an `intent_rank` ordering (one LLM call's output) and, under `broadest_first`, of the loose-last band (its retrieval scores do not round-trip). A candidate that promotes to `no_data` drops from the set; fewer than two survivors makes the part `definite` (one survivor) or `no_data` (zero), carrying a part-scoped `candidates_collapsed_definite` / `candidates_collapsed_no_data` warning naming how many candidates were posted and what remains The engine never accepts a posted `NoData`/`Unsupported` payload on this or any door -- posted state is input to verification, never output (`region/resubmit.py`) | C139   | Three refusal classes on this door: a malformed entry (`EngineInputError` -- `shape_mismatch`, `invalid_role_ref`, `open_role_unavailable`, ...) fails the whole request 4xx with the pointer re-pointed to `/input/entries/{i}/...`; a resolution outcome (`no_data`/`unsupported` from a promote) is a 200 part, siblings unaffected; infra or a timeout, a resubmit's member walk included, fails the whole request 5xx/504. A malformed-entry refusal is deterministic when several entries are invalid. A static check that needs no I/O (a placeholder entry's `code`, or a candidates entry's `candidates` length against the deployment's ceiling) always outranks a refusal only a live resolve can discover, and within either class the lowest entry index wins | C38    | With include_observations true, observations carries the preview for one committed definite spec joined on spec_id; the buffered path embeds the same payload in resolved. A bulk preview leaves out a measure whose points do not convert and names it in an `observations_converted_scope_unsupported` warning, or omits the preview with that reason when no measure converts. A read that fails or crosses the deadline while a bulk preview converts omits the preview with `observations_bulk_fetch_failed` or `observations_timed_out`. A scope that comes back refused (e.g. unsupported_peers_anchor_type, children_anchor_required) or failed (a graph transport error) is dropped rather than emitted with an error; no event at all fires for a part whose identity binds no observationAbout place. entry/dataset_resubmit.py) -- each scheduling build_place_options_events on a worker thread via asyncio.to_thread. enrichment._stream_slot_options awaits the resulting task under its own PLACE_OPTIONS_BUDGET_S (1.0s), bounded by whichever of that budget or the shared request deadline is sooner, so a slow derivation never holds up done; a task past its deadline is cancelled and dropped silently, with no warning and no synthesized event. Scope cut: for a Spec whose identity binds more than one observationAbout dcid, only the first bound anchor gets peers/children events -- a second or third bound place in a multi-place identity is not prefetched. Issue: none. |

| C129   | The envelope carries a composed rendered_sentence/sentence_plan pair, composed over every part's own sentence tokens in query order; absent (both keys omitted) exactly when no contributing part carries a sentence of its own. A part folded into an earlier part's spec (C51a's binding_folded) contributes nothing and is excluded first; the earlier part's heading and byline tokens then carry `part_index` null, since every part folded into it states them, and its departures keep its index. A response with exactly one contributing part composes to that part's own sentence followed, for a definite part, by its departures and its assumptions clause (C193). A definite sole part goes through C130 with one part: every qualifier it states agrees with itself and is stated once, so its connectives, qualifiers, terminal `.`, and byline carry `part_index` null while its phrase (possessive lead, what, unit, and how tokens) keeps its own index. A sole part that is not definite (candidates/no_data/unsupported) is mirrored verbatim, every token under its own `part_index`, its departures already in its own plan. Each definite part's departures (C170) follow the composed headings and byline in part order, as `segment="departure"` text tokens carrying that part's own `part_index`, each after a departure-segment space token; this is the one sentence that writes out a definite part's departures                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | implemented |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| C130   | When every contributing part is definite, their headings join into one sentence. A qualifier (where, origin, when, or source; origin is identified by role=='origin', since SentenceAxis has no 'origin' member) that every part states exactly once and alike, matched on text, connective, `binding_ref`, role, and `entity_refs`, is stated once after the joined phrases, in the first part's order and the family's wording ("USA's health ODA and Britain's education ODA to Ethiopia"), with `part_index` null; a client's edit to it applies to every part. With two or more parts, a possessive lead every part shares opens the sentence once ("Brazil's total population and nominal GDP"), and parts whose phrases differ only in their lead join the leads, each under its own `part_index`, before one shared noun with `part_index` null ("USA's and Britain's health ODA to Ethiopia"). Entity tokens keep their case; a later phrase's first word is lowered only when it is plain Title Case. The byline follows the joined heading once, unindexed; an empty byline placeholder every part shares appears once, unindexed, and otherwise under each part's own index. Any part that is not definite blocks the join outright (C131)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | implemented |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |

comma (a period span, ", 1960 to 2024"): that would read as a list item inside the join, so the response composes one sentence per part (C131) A definite part's sentence is its own `spec.resolved.sentence_plan` verbatim followed by its departures and its assumptions clause (C193), every token carrying `part_index: i`. A mixed resolution opens with the coverage tokens C194 states. Nothing is parenthesised ("Answered 1 of 2 parts. Brazil's total population, 1960 to 2024. GDP has 2 readings. ..."). A part that is not definite composes as C133 states A mixed resolution with no definite part carries no opener, and a resolution whose parts share one status carries none | implemented | Pinned by `tests/render/test_compose_envelope.py` (`test_a_mixed_answer_opens_with_how_many_parts_it_answered`, whose `with-a-folded-part` row counts a folded part as answered, and `test_a_mixed_answer_with_no_definite_part_has_no_opener`) and the sentence golden (`weo-008`, `weo-044`) |
| C132   | The joined composition (C130) mints the Oxford connective(s) joining the phrases (`" and "`, or Oxford `", "` before a final `", and "` at three or more) and the sentence's own closing `"."`, minted once over the whole composition unless it already ends in `.`, `!`, or `?`. Both carry `part_index` null, as does the unindexed `" "` the per-part composition (C131) mints between sentences. Every other token in the joined sentence (each phrase's own words, a lead, a shared or inline qualifier's connective and slot token, the byline) has its text copied from a contributing part's own plan, never composer prose; its position relative to that part's own plan is not preserved (a shared counterpart moves after every phrase, "... for health and ... for education to Ethiopia") | C133   | Beside other parts (C131), a candidates part contributes its `variable_text` as an editable `what`-axis slot, then its own sentence with its count lowered into a predicate ("GDP has 2 readings. ..."); an unsupported part contributes the slot, then a text token stating its detail; a no_data part whose reason quotes the variable (variable_not_resolved, denominator_not_available, no_matching_slice, binding_mismatch, role_incompatible) contributes an optional prefix text token, the slot, a text token stating the outcome, then the rest of its own plan. Every other no_data part, and a part with no `variable_text`, contributes its own sentence verbatim, since it names what was checked itself. Every token carries that part's own part_index. A client derives the slot's styling and any affordance it hangs off it (a candidate menu, a retry prompt) from `parts[i].status`; no token carries a status field of its own                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | implemented |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| C141   | Every `Part`, whatever its status, carries `restatement`: a `ParsedPart` reproducing this part's own resolution inputs (entities, resolved time window, unit text, operations, open roles, and as `sources` the part's source mentions, each with the reference it resolved to, or for a part with none the declared dataset its identity binds as its source or its no-data run settled on, as one included mention), as if it were posted fresh to the `parsed` door. It is canonical, not verbatim: for a raw_text part, `time_text` is the phrase this door's own time grammar re-parses back into the identical window, not the user's original words, since date extraction keeps only the structured window. The guarantee is reproducing the same resolution inputs and `spec_id`, never the same wording. A part folded into an earlier part's spec still echoes its own inputs, never the representative's. It is null on a `dataset_resubmit` entry's `identity`/`candidates`/`placeholder` kinds, all of which post an identity or stub rather than a phrase; a `dataset_resubmit` `parsed` entry, genuinely re-resolved from its own posted `ParsedPart`, carries a real one like any parsed-door part. A leg bound on a directional role axis (origin/destination) echoes that axis, per entity, as the `role_text` a client can post straight back: `from` for the origin axis and `to` for the destination one, chosen from the same lexicon `role_axis_for_text` reads. A name on both axes echoes one row per axis. A leg's own open roles echo as `open_roles` words, `origin` or `destination`, and the axes its operations open ride on the operations. It is null when the leg's direction came from its own wording alone and no door-supplied role named the axis, and when one of the leg's own inputs sits outside `ParsedPart`'s field bounds. One documented gap: an open-start/closed-end or reversed time window has no phrase in this grammar, so `time_text` comes back null rather than a wrong one.                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | implemented |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| C141a  | Every `Part` carries `entity_refs`: one `{text, refs}` entry per `restatement.entities` row, in row order, `text` the row's text and `refs` holding the refs the part's answer binds for the row's identity (C12) once its places have settled, so a self-flow's two rows state the same refs and "aid from Congo to Congo" states each end's. A place gives its node. A group, continent or subregion bound as itself gives its node or the total its dataset publishes for it ("G7" bound as `GroupOfSeven`). One bound as its members gives the set's own ref first, its node, else that total, else its registry id ("G7" read as members gives `GroupOfSeven` and then each member), followed by the members, sorted, so a client matches the set the text named by its ref. A text naming a whole population gives the parties it names that the answer binds. `refs` is `[]` for an ambiguous text (C50c), a text that resolved to no node or to one the answer does not bind, a group or a set read as its members in a part refused before its places were bound, and every text of a soft-deadline placeholder part. In a part refused before its places were bound, a text that resolved to one node gives that node. A candidates part states the elected reading's binding, and a part folded into an earlier part's spec states its own. The field sits beside `restatement`, which stays a `ParsedPart` and posts back verbatim. It is `[]` when `restatement` is null or names no entity.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | implemented | Additive response field; it does not alter `restatement`, the request models or any registry code. Pinned by `tests/engine/test_part_restatement.py` (`TestEntityRefs`, the folded part, the verbatim wire round trip), `tests/engine/test_group_resolution.py` (a group bound as its total or its members) and `tests/engine/test_render_e2e.py` (an ambiguous text, a part refused before its places were bound).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |

| C42    | A resolution's parts is a non-empty list, one entry per variable asked, in query order; each entry is a complete outcome carrying its own status, payload, and `operations`. | C42a   | A raw_text question extract classifies as `catalogue`, `out_of_scope` or `requires_analysis` and that names no measure returns straight after extract, with no recall, bind or layer-2 lookup; one that names a measure resolves, so text in the query cannot decline a data question. Its only graph reads are the entity resolver's place lookup (`node_arcs_many`, `node_names_many`, `resolve_by_arc`), made only when the query names a place; a question naming none reads nothing from the graph. `scope.place` is the one country the entity resolver confirms among the places the query names, in one resolver call under the request deadline that sends every distinct name; no place, a city, a region, an organisation, two countries, or a resolver failure or timeout leave it null, and a failure or timeout keeps the answer out of the response cache. A question naming more than `max_entity_occurrences` place occurrences or `max_entities` distinct names answers with its kind and no places, so `scope.place` is null and the resolver is not called; a resolution past either cap refuses `request_invalid` (400). Layer 1 stores and serves these answers. The parsed and dataset_resubmit doors run no extract and always answer a resolution (`test_extract_clears_the_places_of_a_question_past_a_cap_it_does_not_resolve`). | C42b   | On the raw_text door each part carries the operations extract admitted for its variable, in query order, and its restatement carries them too. An operation's phrase reaches only that variable's bind, rendered in the dropped-qualifier block after the request's dropped phrases, so a `requested_all` claim on it leaves the slot it names open. An operation over `category` whose phrase names exactly one slot's declared plain name (flow, sector, instrument, modality, singular or plural) opens that slot whether or not bind claims it, so no declared default narrows it; a slot bind gave a value keeps it, and a phrase naming two ("top 5 sectors by aid flows") opens neither. An operation raises no `query_qualifier_dropped` warning and adds no sentence note. A dropped qualifier an admitted operation overlaps (one phrase holds the other's words in order) loses, from both ends, the operation's words, the role noun of an origin or destination axis it runs over, function and date words, and each run of words that is an entity's text or span or the role span of an entity holding a flow role (beside the entity "United States", "top 5 donors in fragile states" keeps "fragile states"; "top 5 donantes a Kenia" beside Kenya with the span "Kenia" leaves nothing, and neither does "top 5 recipients funded by Norway" beside Norway with the role span "funded by"). Role spans are validated before the trim reads them, so a role span validation clears credits no words: in "top 5 donors in fragile states from Norway to Kenya", Kenya's role span "fragile states" crosses Norway on another axis, is cleared, and "fragile states" stays an unapplied qualifier with its warning and sentence note. A span or role span the model fills with query words that name no place and no cue, and that validation accepts, is credited. A category operation's words include a slot noun only when its phrase opens that slot, so a rank phrased "Which got the most" leaves "flows got the most aid" of "Which flows got the most aid in Kenya in 2022". It leaves `dropped_qualifiers` when nothing is left or the rest is one variable's words, a slot noun among them included ("top 5 aid flows" beside the variable "aid flows"); otherwise the rest replaces it and warns unless the answer applied it, so "top 5 donors of standard grants" beside the variable "ODA standard grants" warns for "standard grants" when bind asks rather than electing. When a declared default fixes a slot no operation opened, and the variable, the single-variable raw query, a dropped phrase or an operation phrase names that slot's plain name, singular or plural, the part carries the `slot_defaulted` warning, whose message names the slot and the default ("No flow was named, so the answer covers official development assistance, the default flow."). The warning belongs to the door that runs the election: the first answer's bind elects the default, the sentence does not repeat it, and a resubmit, which posts the default and elects nothing, raises none. "aid to Kenya" names no slot and does not warn. A dropped phrase that names the slot and otherwise holds only function words and the variable's words ("flows" beside the variable "aid") then raises no `query_qualifier_dropped` warning, while "flows got the most aid" keeps its own. The layer-2 cache key carries each slot with a declared default that the raw query names. A qualifier no admitted operation overlaps stays whole. An admitted forecast's target years, the dates lying wholly after today's year and naming no year past 2200, leave its own variable's dates, so its part resolves over the years the data holds: when every variable forecasts they leave `dates`, and otherwise each forecasting variable resolves without them while the others keep them ("forecast aid to Kenya and Kenya's projected population in 2050": the aid part has no `time_text`, the population part "2050"). Each admitted forecast keeps the year it projects to as `target_year`, read before the drop: the latest year after today's and no later than 2200 that its own phrase names, or else that the query's dates name. A year is a run of exactly four digits, whatever sits beside it ("预测2050年", "FY2050", "the 2050s"), and a number past the bound is no year, so "forecast top 5000 aid to Ethiopia in 2030" targets 2030, and "forecast aid to Ethiopia to 2300" carries no target and keeps 2300 in its dates. "forecast aid to Ethiopia to 2050" with the phrase "forecast" carries `target_year` 2050 and its restatement, posted back alone through the parsed door or as a dataset_resubmit parsed entry, keeps it. A forecast admission discards drops nothing. A variable the model reworded only by reordering takes the query's wording, which the part shows as `variable_text`: a span whose words other than a, an, the, of, who, which, that, is, are, was and were are exactly the variable's, each as often, in a different order, with only whitespace or a hyphen between two of them ("extreme poverty rate" in "global rate of extreme poverty" reads "rate of extreme poverty"; "youth unemployment rate" in "unemployment rate and youth unemployment" stays). The layer-2 cache key includes each variable's operations in order. An operation whose `over` is `origin` or `destination` opens that axis on every door (`search.models.asked_open_axes`), whether or not bind claims it. A family with a by-creditor twin then elects the twin, whose answer declares the role and keeps the operation, and the identity states the axis in `open_roles`. The operation leaves the part, with a `query_qualifier_dropped` warning, only when the answer declares no role on that axis (`region.result.with_runnable_operations`) | implemented | No gap. `extract.py` (`_restore_query_word_order`, `_admit_operations`, `_occurs_in`, `_target_forecasts`, `_drop_forecast_years`, `_validate_role_spans`, `_applied_phrases`, `_trim_qualifiers_to_what_operations_leave`), `qualifier_match.py` (`slot_noun_named`), `bind_repair.py` (`defaulted_slots_named`, `declared_default_slots_named`), `extract.slot_default_answers`, `region/phase_election.py` (`_repair_bindings`, which raises the warning), `entry/raw_text.py` (`_ExtractionPrep.per_variable_operations`, `_ExtractionPrep.per_variable_date_requests`), `fanout.py` (`_bind_phrases`, `_leg_restatement`), `models/request.py` (`asked_open_axes`), `region/phase_rank_bind.py` (`_open_operation_slots`), `response_cache_keys.layer2_key`; pinned by `tests/engine/test_operations_e2e.py` (`test_a_reposted_forecast_part_keeps_its_target_year`, `test_an_invalid_role_span_leaves_its_qualifier_unapplied_with_a_note`), `tests/engine/test_extract.py`, `tests/engine/test_operations_echo.py`, `tests/engine/test_requested_all_validation.py`, `tests/engine/test_bind_repair.py` (`TestDefaultedSlotsNamed`), `tests/engine/test_response_cache_signals.py` and `tests/engine/test_response_cache_keys.py` (`test_operations_change_layer2_key`, `test_raw_text_naming_a_defaulted_slot_alone_changes_layer2_key`). |
| C42c   | On the raw_text door extract states, for each entity, whether the question asks for the members of the region or group it names (`ExtractedEntity.members`, default false): "African countries", "countries in Africa" and "EU member states" are the set's name with `members` true, and "Africa", "Africa as a whole" and a single place have it false. The language model reads the wording and the engine matches no words. An entity with `members` true binds as a parsed entity with `members: true` does (C12). Two extracted mentions of one text hold one reading, and the mention that asks for the members decides it. The part's `restatement` states the flag, and the layer-2 cache key holds it, so the two readings of one set keep separate entries. | implemented | No gap. `extract.py` (`ExtractedEntity.members`), `entry/raw_text.py` (`_prepare_extraction`), `response_cache_keys.py` (`layer2_key`). Tests: `tests/engine/test_place_set_examples.py` (`test_raw_text_that_asks_for_africas_members_binds_them`, `test_raw_text_ranking_africas_recipients_binds_its_members`), `tests/engine/test_extract.py` (`test_the_extraction_schema_offers_members_on_an_entity`) and `tests/engine/test_response_cache_keys.py` (`test_an_entitys_members_flag_alone_changes_layer2_key`). |
| C42d   | On the raw_text door each extracted variable the query compares with another is marked separate, as a parsed part posted with `separate: true` is (C51a), after extraction and with no model call (`source_spans.read_sources`, called from `entry/raw_text.py` `_prepare_extraction`). A variable is located by the content words of its text, less `FUNCTION_WORDS` (`source_spans.locate_variables`). A variable holding a word no other variable holds starts at that word's first occurrence past the location before it, else at its first occurrence anywhere, and widens left over its own words and function words beside it. A variable whose every word another holds starts at the first of its words past the location before it. Each location reads on, each word once, until it has read all its words or meets a word it read already, a word only other variables hold, another location or a comparison connective. No location crosses a comparison connective, and a variable whose every word another holds that meets one before reading all its words starts again at its first word past it. "health aid" from "compare aid to health and education" is located at "aid to health", "health ODA" and "basic health ODA" from "compare health ODA with basic health ODA" at each occurrence in turn, "health ODA" from "How much ODA did Kenya receive? Compare health ODA with education ODA" at "health ODA", and "education ODA" and "health ODA", in that order, from "health ODA vs education ODA" at each. A variable with no word outside the other locations stays unmarked. Two located variables are marked when a comparison connective governs them by the rule a part's own codes are joined by (`qualifier_match.governing_connectives`, `qualifier_match.joins`), with the content words two or more variables hold read as function words: "versus" or "vs" between them, or "compare" or "compared" before both. A comparison connective that governs a located variable also marks every located variable after it, whatever joins them. "compare aid to health and education", "compare health aid with education aid in Kenya", "ODA to health vs. ODA to education" (in either variable order), "health versus education aid", "compare health ODA with basic health ODA" and "For ODA to Kenya, compare health with education" mark both variables, and "health and education aid" and "health aid and education aid" mark neither. The layer-2 cache key holds the marks. After the layer-2 lookup the door logs, once, the raw-query words no part, operation, entity, date or unit text claims and that are neither function words nor a comparison connective that marked a variable, as the INFO line `raw_query_coverage unclaimed=<words>`; the response carries no field for them | implemented | No gap. `source_spans.py` (`locate_variables`, `read_sources`, `unclaimed_words`), `entry/raw_text.py` (`_prepare_extraction`, `_log_unclaimed_words`), `response_cache_flow.py` (`CacheScope.lookup_parsed`). Tests: `tests/engine/test_source_spans.py` (`test_a_comparison_connective_governing_two_located_variables_marks_both`, `test_variables_align_to_the_query_words`, `test_the_raw_door_carries_the_marks_to_the_legs_and_the_cache_lookup`, `test_the_raw_door_logs_the_words_no_part_claims`, `test_a_marked_variable_keys_layer2_apart_from_the_unmarked_extraction`). |

| C45 | A candidates part's candidates field is a list of 2..max_candidates structurally distinct specs, set by the status rule (`region.status_rule`) over the elected reading P, its admissible alternatives and whether each has data where the question asks: yes, no or unknown. A read that found no data, or none in the requested years, is a no only when it covered every place the part names; a read of fewer places, and a failed read, leave it unknown, and an unknown reading stays in play. With data on P, P comes first, then its rivals in menu order: readings of P's population type and measured property that differ from it only on a stat type, price basis or denominator the question leaves open, and readings the bind model nominated. A value the bind model drafts on a property P has no slot for holds every reading (C189): a rival that does not apply it is not offered, and P leaves when a rival applies it. Two or more such rivals are the candidates, without P. One such rival answers alone when it reads P's measure in the asked direction: a reading differing from P on an open axis, or a registry twin of P. A lone reading of another measure (nominated), of the other direction, or offered on no basis does not answer, and the part refuses `qualifier_not_bound` naming the value, with P as flagged_match, no gate and no alternate_match. With no data on P, an equivalent reading answers; else a sole sibling with data differing from P only on the measurement qualifier answers, and several are offered in menu order without P. Every reading the rule weighs comes from the pass's own pool (C51e). A place named without a direction whose data sits under both directions offers P under each. A family with no total whose request names no breakdown that serves a member offers each of its breakdown signatures (C196). A pinned part never produces candidates. `ordering` is `intent_rank` on every first answer | implemented | Pinned by `tests/engine/test_status_rule.py`, `tests/engine/test_place_context_resubmit_equality.py`, `tests/engine/test_availability.py` (`TestMenuAvailabilityRead`) and `tests/engine/test_zero_observation_election.py` (`test_a_stray_value_binds_every_candidate_the_elected_reading_included`) and `tests/engine/test_un_tables_serving.py`. |

| C196 | A part whose elected family holds no total, under bindings that open no slot, with members that break down by several breakdown signatures, each the set of unbound slot properties a member asserts, answers candidates: one copy of the elected shape per signature, its identity carrying the bound slots and that signature alone (`phase_place_materialise._settle_breakdown`). Opened slots that leave no member, every member also asserting a slot the request did not open, offer the signatures that hold every opened slot the same way, so each candidate carries the breakdown the question named. The copies are ordered by their members with data at the places the part names in the years it reads, then by their recalled members, and capped at max_candidates, with candidates.truncated true when the cap cuts a signature (C47). When the cap cuts one, the family's members that recall did not return are read at those places too, so a breakdown holding data only in such members ranks as a recalled one would. The first copy is the elected reading and each other carries basis `breakdown`. Each candidate's served series, coverage and download describe what a mint of its definition serves (`grounding.check_breakdown_settled`), so a resubmit of its identity answers it under the same spec_id. A copy left alone once the others drop answers definite with the family_total_absent warning and closest_match departure of C184 | implemented | Pinned by `tests/engine/test_un_tables_serving.py` (`test_a_series_with_no_total_and_two_breakdowns_offers_each_as_a_candidate`, `test_a_cap_below_the_breakdown_count_truncates_the_candidates`, `test_the_cap_keeps_the_breakdown_holding_data_in_the_years_asked_for`, `test_a_breakdown_no_member_holds_alone_answers_by_the_breakdown_holding_it`, `test_a_resubmit_of_a_breakdown_answer_keeps_its_spec_id_and_names_its_breakdown`). |

| C50c   | For entity_ambiguous only, no_data.ambiguous_entities carries one group per ambiguous wording and flow end the request gave it (empty for every other reason), each with the graph candidates it could name (ref, label, kind), `ref` holding the candidate's dcid. Every group's text equals some restatement.entities[i].text on the same part. A subject-role entity_ambiguous part (e.g. "GDP of Congo", no role_text posted) carries a real restatement instead of null. The part also carries no_data.options, one per (group, candidate) in query order, each with the wording, the candidate's label, and a one-part parsed input built from the part's parsed inputs (variable text, entities with role_text, time, unit and sources, operations) pinning that candidate on the group's end and every entity that resolved to one node; named groups, sets read as members, other ambiguous wordings and the wording's other end stay unpinned. Each ambiguous row of an option and of the restatement posts the words its resolution read as `text` and the extracted name as `name` (C12), the name left out when it equals the words; a chosen row keeps the words and posts the candidate's dcid, its identity, with no name, and so does every row the option pins to the node it resolved to. The group's text, entity_terms and mentions quote those same words: a span that differs from the name only in case, or that the query does not hold, is quoted and posted as the name. Hence the parsed door settles every unchosen row as the refusal did: "aid from Congo to Congo" posts two "Congo" rows, under two names or one, and asks each end again apart, and "Congolese" read as Congo posts `{text: Congolese, name: Congo}`, which asks the Congo choice again. A candidate that is a group's own node, on a row asked for its members, posts the group's registered name with no dcid (C50d). Posting an option's data_query unchanged resolves that wording. options is built whether or not the part carries a restatement; an entity whose direction the question leaves unnamed carries no role_text. options is empty past the ParsedPart bounds. The part's sentence plan marks each ambiguous wording as a slot token carrying the role the request gave it, no entity_refs and no binding_ref, so the composed sentence stamps it with the part's index and a client hangs the candidate menu on it; the rendered text is unchanged. Each entity option and each such slot token carry `entity_choice`, the index of their group in no_data.ambiguous_entities, since two groups can share one wording ("aid from Congo to Congo" asks "Congo" for each end); a client groups an entity menu by it. Every other option and token leaves it null. Where one wording names two groups, the message and the sentence quote each in the same words and tell them apart outside the quotes: by the axis word of each group's role (`"Congo" (from) and "Congo" (to) each match more than one entity: ...`), or by order where the roles are equal or absent (`The first "Congo" and the second "Congo" each match ...`); a wording written once reads unqualified. | C51e   | A part's first pass, our pass, reads our resolver's candidates alone, a superseded variable among them standing as its successor (C185); the UN resolver's candidates are not in it. The UN resolve and the derivation of the UN pool's shapes run beside our pass as one task (`ResolveState.un_leg`), each shape stamped with its pool (`ShapeDraft.pool`). A part runs a UN pass over the UN pool's shapes when its own pass ends no_data for a reason other than entity_not_resolved, entity_ambiguous, entity_set_too_large or entity_role_not_admitted, or answers definite or candidates on its resolution without party words on a pick its binder rated "none of the shapes" above (`BindResult.none_first`, a doubted answer), and when the part refused no source_ambiguous, names no source_code (C190), has a source scope that allows a UN System Data Commons provenance or none (C78b), and did not settle the answer by its exclusions. The UN pass reads the same recall and the same resolved entities, less the shape our pass elected when our pass found it without observations or answered on it doubted, so a UN series our pass recalled under a superseded variable is not elected again. Each pass's candidates are scored by one resolver and meet its own score floor, SEARCH_UNKNOWN_SCORE_THRESHOLD for ours and SEARCH_UN_UNKNOWN_SCORE_THRESHOLD for the UN pool, both 0.65, and each pass's bind menu holds its own pool's first SEARCH_BIND_TOP_K shapes in rank order, each showing its origin. The UN pass waits for the UN leg at most SEARCH_UN_FIRST_PASS_WAIT_S (1.5 s) from the start of our derivation, and ends 3 seconds before the request deadline, so our answer still returns in time; a part our pass answers undoubted never waits for the leg. The UN pass's answer stands when it is definite or candidates, except when the UN pass's binder also rated "none of the shapes" above its pick on a doubted answer of ours, and except over our pass holding an alternate_match (gate only_alternate_has_data) when the UN pass's elected shape scores in the retrieval_score_weak band. Of two refusals, the UN pass's stands when it carries an alternate_match and ours carries none, and when ours is variable_not_resolved, the UN pass's is any other reason and the UN pass elected a shape whose measured score is present and outside the retrieval_score_weak band. The elected shape is the evidence of the match, whatever the reason: a UN refusal with no elected shape, such as denominator_not_available on a count recalled for a rate, leaves ours standing. A weak UN match says no more than ours: "measles incidence rate" is not a burden-of-disease rate. Otherwise our answer stands with its reason, including when the UN pass fails. The trailing observations, slot-options and place-options reads of the answer that does not stand are cancelled. A UN leg that failed or was not in, and a UN pass that fails upstream, runs out of time or has no time to start, add un_system_unavailable (C107a); any other failure adds nothing. The part's timing_ms and model usage sum both passes. On SSE, frame, shape and step events describe our pass. Each part logs one `gap_fill` line naming our pass's status, the pool of its elected shape, what the UN pass did, whether our pass's menu held the UN pool (`un_merged`) and whether its binder doubted its answer (`doubted`). With SEARCH_UN_ON_FIRST_MENU (default off), our pass's menu also holds the UN pool's shapes: only a part whose best shape scores below 0.7 takes them, waiting for the UN leg up to SEARCH_UN_FIRST_PASS_WAIT_S, and a part with a stronger shape never takes the UN pool on its pass, however soon the leg finishes. The UN shapes meet the same admissibility, the UN floor, and the same seats in score order beside ours; the status rule reads a UN shape and ours of one measure as two measures, so the other is listed only when the model nominates it. A merged pass that ends no_data still runs the UN pass over the UN pool, and a doubted answer on a merged pass runs it over the UN shapes that took no seat on our menu (`ResolveState.capped_shapes`), since its binder rated "none of the shapes" above only the shapes it saw; when every UN shape took a seat, it runs none | implemented | Pinned by `tests/engine/test_pool_election.py` (`test_a_doubted_answer_of_ours_gives_way_to_an_undoubted_un_answer`, `test_a_doubted_answer_excludes_its_own_series_from_the_un_pool`, `test_a_un_refusal_on_a_matched_variable_stands_over_ours_matching_none`, `test_a_un_refusal_before_election_leaves_ours_matching_none`, `test_the_dropped_answer_of_a_gap_fill_cancels_its_trailing_tasks`, `test_a_doubted_answer_on_a_merged_menu_runs_the_un_pass_over_its_unseated_shapes`, `test_a_doubted_answer_reaches_the_un_shape_its_menu_left_unseated`) and `tests/engine/test_recall_transfer.py` (`test_our_pass_holds_no_candidate_of_the_un_resolver`, `test_each_passs_candidates_are_scored_by_one_resolver`, `test_the_un_pass_does_not_elect_the_transferred_shape_our_pass_found_empty`, `test_a_un_resolve_that_never_answers_leaves_a_transferred_answer_intact`, `test_with_the_un_origin_off_nothing_is_replaced_and_no_un_pass_runs`). |

| C189   | A code the request names, or a resubmit posts, on a property the answering shape's slots hold is pinned: a definite read that does not apply it refuses `qualifier_not_bound` on every door (raw text, parsed, resubmit), with the read as flagged_match and the code named once as an unbound term, by its dcid when it has no name, and no departure beside it. A single surviving alternate is judged on its own shape, bindings and label, its slot-value words included, so one that binds the code, or a descendant of it, answers. A word of the variable that names, by its graph label, a value on a property the answering shape's slots hold ("female" for `gender` Female), as that shape or another on the bind menu offers it, is such a code whether or not the bind model lists it as unplaced, unless the answering shape's own label or population type names it; the shape answers only when its final bindings bind it or a value below it. A value word on a property the answering shape lacks is left to the bind model's judgement of the measure: it refuses when the model lists it as unplaced, and otherwise the answer departs `measure_not_applied` (`query_qualifier_dropped`) on it, unless the property is a code system the index declares, whose words the code reading judges. A value the bind model drafts on a property the elected shape has no slot for is required of every reading, judged by values alone (a word in a member's name does not meet it): a reading meets it when its bindings bind it, or every member it reads holds it or a value below it. The values drafted on one property are a set, met by membership as a set bound on a slot reads: every member the reading reads holds a value of the set or one below it. An alternate binds its slot on that property to the drafted values its members hold, a set when they hold several, so "male and female" on a shape with no sex slot offers each reading whose members hold either sex, a by-sex family serving both. A reading with a member holding a value outside the set, or none on the property, does not meet it. A reading that does not meet it is not offered, the elected one included (C45), and when none meets it the part refuses `qualifier_not_bound` naming the drafted values as one term, with the elected read as flagged_match and no departure beside it. Overlapping value labels are read longest first. It names the StatVars the packaged code index records for it under each provenance holding it (`CodeIndex.source_code_members`, read from the `sourceIndicatorCode` arcs of its listed rows), and it compiles at the door after the part's sources, against the provenances they allow (`source_code.compile_part_code`, in `entry.parsed.interpret_parts` and `entry.raw_text._prepare_extraction`). A code no allowed provenance holds refuses source_code_not_held, its message naming the cause: the index holds no such code, naming every source whose codes it holds, or the part's sources leave out every provenance holding it, naming those; a code two allowed provenances hold refuses source_code_ambiguous, with the code and those provenances in `NoData.ambiguous_sources` and one option per provenance in `NoData.options` (C50e), and the part answers once its `sources` names one; a code whose StatVar its provenance gives another code too (`CodeIndex.shared_codes`: IDS gives one net-flow StatVar DT.NFL.IMFC.CD and DT.NFL.MIDA.CD) refuses source_code_shares_variable, its message naming those codes. Each of these door refusals carries the code in `NoData.source_terms` and runs no step. Otherwise the code's StatVars are the part's recall in place of the embedding resolve (`interpret.recall`), admitted by the request's catalogue as recalled hits are, and shape derivation, the twin election and bind run on them as on a recalled pool, so `open_roles: ["creditor"]` elects DT.TDS.DEGG.CD's by-creditor variable and no open role its aggregate. Bind binds every constraint value the code's StatVars all share as a sole option (`bound_values.bind_member_values`), so the minted identity elects those StatVars and no broader family, and a variable text that is only the code reaches the bind model as its StatVars' graph name. A code part refuses source_code_not_held, carrying the code in `NoData.source_terms` and the part's warnings, model usage and source scope, wherever it would refuse variable_not_resolved (`ResolveState.no_data`): its message names the cause, that the catalogue or graph serves none of the code's StatVars when no shape derives from them, and that the code's series cannot answer the part as asked otherwise, a bind that asks among them. So does a part one of whose Specs mints an identity whose Datasets mint serves StatVars outside the code's, or ones the code index does not resolve in memory (`pipeline._within_source_code`). Each entry of a Spec's `resolved.stat_vars.source_codes` states in `exact` whether the Spec is the series its provenance files under that code and no other (`source_code.stands_for_code`, called from `spec_build._resolved_stat_vars`): the Spec reads its observations from that provenance alone (`resolved.facets.provenance.ref`), the provenance gives none of the code's StatVars another code, every StatVar the Spec serves is the code's, and the StatVars a Datasets mint of its identity serves, its antichain-minimal members (`bound_values.served_members`), are known and lie within the code's StatVars (`bound_values.elects_within`). A Spec found by the measure's words gets the flag by the same computation as one a code part answers with, and a false `exact` is left off the wire. A code part gets no UN pass (C51e). The variable text leaves out the code's words (`source_code.without_code_words`), the restatement echoes the code, and posting it mints the same `spec_id`. | C69a   | `open_roles` is a sorted, de-duplicated list of flow axes (`origin`, `destination`), omitted from the projection when empty or absent. An open role denotes every member of the role with data, one series per member; a role in neither `entities` nor `open_roles` denotes the total over its members. The two are different asks and mint different spec_ids, and an identity without open roles keeps its spec_id. `BaseIdentity` refuses a role that `entities` also binds, and a pinned `stat_var` shape carries none | implemented |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| C69b   | Search writes `open_roles` as the axes the part asked for, by an `open_roles` word or an operation over the axis, that the elected role class declares (`retrieve.Materialised.open_axes`). The election reads every axis asked for, and `open_roles` states the axes the elected members serve. Where the code index elects the family among the datasets publishing it, `open_roles` are the open axes that joined that election (`code_index.members.DatasetFamily.role_keys`), the one set under which the mint is sure to elect the same dataset and members; an axis the election set aside is omitted even where an elected member declares a role on it. An open axis joins the election's role keys softly (`statvar_members.electing_role_keys`). The members that can place it are those the bound roles alone elect and their role twins, the members that hold the constraint values of one of them under another role set. The bound roles elect the members of the smallest role set only where every other role set left contains it and the constraint properties of each of its members are among those of every other member left (`statvar_members.elect_role_class`); otherwise they tie, and every member the tie leaves can place the axis. The largest subset of the open axes that one such member declares together with the bound roles joins, so a by-creditor twin displaces its total. On an equal-size tie between different subsets no open axis joins, and an axis only other members declare never joins: a by-recipient series at constraint values no elected total holds is the twin of none. The family then elects as if the role were unnamed, and where its elected members declare no role on the axis the identity omits it and the part warns as C12a states. A resubmit writes the same elected axes. An open role a posted identity names is a promise of one series per member, and the identity is the whole definition: where the elected members declare no role on that axis, the resubmit refuses `open_role_unavailable`, 422, at `/input/entries/{i}/identity/open_roles` (`promote_materialise._refuse_unserved_open_roles`), checked once after the last materialisation and before any grounding read. A candidates entry's candidate refuses the same way, at `/input/entries/{i}/candidates/{j}/identity/open_roles`. On the parsed door an open role is a request: where it goes unserved the identity omits it and the part warns `query_qualifier_dropped` with a `breakdown_not_applied` departure (C12a). An identity minted without an election carries the open axes asked for: an `unsupported` part's definition keeps the axes the part asks for that no place binds on the parsed door (`phase_shared._unsupported_definition`) and the posted `open_roles` on the resubmit door (`promote_materialise._resubmit_unsupported_definition`). A related measure's identity carries no open role (`related.related_measures`), and the same-shape merge requires equal `open_roles` (`same_shape._check_merge_gates`) | implemented |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |

| C75a   | resolved.shape.member_digest: lowercase sha256 hex of the RFC 8785 (JCS) canonical JSON of the sorted list of bare dcids (no "dcid:" prefix) of the family a Datasets mint of the Spec's definition resolves, before antichain reduction: every row of the family the code index resolves for the spec's identity, or every member the mint's graph walk confirms when Search's own walk of the identity read every candidate (`MemberGroup.stated_family`). Null, so absent from the wire, when Search did not settle that family for the definition, because the answer stands on recalled members (the StatVars Search's recall found, unconfirmed by the mint's walk), or its walk proved the family's root from a truncated first page and so cannot name every member. A first answer's budgeted walk can end there where a resubmit of the same definition, which runs the full read (C15), states the digest. A walk's statement counts only for the identity's own query, so a Spec built from relaxed bindings or a set merge states none. The datasets door states the same family in its response trace: trace.family_member_digest is the same digest over the same members, and trace.family_member_count is their number, so the two digests are equal for one definition when both are present and both services run one code index and one graph generation. The trace of a stored version is the one recorded when that version was first stored, so on a mint or a read answered from a stored version an unequal digest means the family has changed since, and a caller re-runs search. This lets a caller verify a persisted plan's stat_var_binding against the population search bound it to without re-running search. The datasets door's trace.resolved_stat_vars lists the members the mint served, which leave out each admitted member a broader member dominates, so a digest over it equals member_digest only for a family with no dominated member. trace.family_member_digest and trace.family_member_count are null when the mint did not read the whole family (a graph walk whose candidate page was truncated and left candidates unread, though a confirmed root proved the family complete) and absent on a manifest stored before the fields existed. A caller reads null and absent alike as unknown. | C78a   | Every provenance ref carries source {ref, label}, the organisation behind it, and data_commons {id, label}, the Data Commons its data comes from, both read from the origin catalogue's provenance table with its label: resolved.facets.provenance (alongside ref only), each series' facet.provenance, each series' source_refs entry and each trace.resolved_sources entry ({ref, label, source, data_commons}). A UN System Data Commons provenance is its own source. A provenance the catalogue does not list carries neither member and takes its graph label, read in one batch over those misses only; a source_refs or resolved_sources entry the graph cannot label is omitted, and its facet.provenance carries no label. Admission drops every provenance without a row, so production reads none
