            | implemented | Pinned by `tests/engine/test_provenance.py` and `tests/engine/test_origin_wire.py`. | C78b   | A part names or excludes sources through its source scope (`ResolveState.scope`): the provenances its mentions allow, compiled before any other input resolves (`source_scope.compile_sources`), and whether it includes any. A parsed part, or the part of a dataset_resubmit parsed entry, posts its mentions in `ParsedPart.sources`, each by its words or by an admitted provenance, Dataset or Source reference. An included mention allows its provenances, a Dataset or Source less its opt-in products unless it publishes nothing else; a part with no include allows every admitted provenance; each excluded mention removes its provenances. An excluded mention whose provenances publish none of the part's recalled shapes removes nothing, and the part warns query_qualifier_dropped quoting its words (`source_choice.idle_exclusion_warnings`) and names it in no outcome as a source that limited the answer, one mark deciding both (`source_choice.with_idle_exclusions`). A shape's publishers are its family's datasets, else its listed rows' provenances, else those the catalogue records for its members, else any provenance but a declared dataset. An included mention none of whose provenances the answer's identities bind as their source (`source_choice.answer_provenances`) served nothing of it, and the answer warns query_qualifier_dropped quoting its words (`source_choice.idle_inclusion_warnings`), marked by the same idle mark (`source_choice.with_idle_inclusions`), so a part naming the CRS and the imputed table that the CRS alone answers warns about the imputed table. Excluded words matching no admitted source stand for no provenance, so they remove nothing and warn the same way, and the restatement keeps the mention with its words and no reference. Included words matching no admitted source refuse source_not_resolved and words naming two unrelated sources, included or excluded, refuse source_ambiguous, each a no_data part naming the words (`NoData.source_terms`, `NoData.ambiguous_sources`, and for source_ambiguous one `NoData.options` entry per candidate with the resolved mentions pinned, C50e). The deprecated `ParsedPart.provenance` and `source_text` fold into one included mention each. The served request schema lists the declared datasets as `provenance`'s values, in rank order, each a `const` with its declared label as `title`, its description and an `x-opt-in` flag. A part's restatement writes `sources` alone, holding the part's mentions, each with the reference it resolved to, so posting it compiles the same scope. A part with no mention restates, as one included mention, the declared dataset its identity binds as its source, where electing an opt-in shape named it, or a no-data part's settled dataset, and restates none when neither names one. Posting the restatement mints the same `spec_id`. A dataset declared opt-in (`DatasetDeclaration.opt_in`) serves a part that names no dataset only on a family (`CodeIndex.family_of`) no other dataset publishes. A named dataset binds the source slot, which hashes as facets.provenance. A part with any mention binds facets.provenance to the provenances its read plan serves, a value for one and a set for several (`spec_build.with_served_provenance`), so a mint or resubmit of the spec_id reads the sources that answered, and `resolved.facets.provenance` reports basis `bound`; the provisional sentence event and an alternate left unread carry no such pin. Shape candidacy is per part and reads the datasets that publish each shape's own family, in rank order (`shape_datasets`): the family the shape's members are recorded in (`shape_family`), which the shape's slot keys do not select again. A part with no scope keeps every shape. Under a scope, a shape of a family the index declares stays when an eligible dataset publishes it (`part_datasets`, through `code_index.members.eligible_among`): of its publishers the scope allows, those not opt-in, else all of them when the scope names a source or no publisher of the family is not opt-in, else none, so excluding a table never opens an opt-in table beside another. A shape whose five-tuple the index lists without a declared dataset (`CodeIndex.listed_statvars`) stays when a listed row's provenance is allowed; any other shape, a table the index does not declare, stays when the scope allows a provenance the index declares no dataset for, whether the part names or only excludes sources. The scoped read serves none of an excluded source's data, so a reading whose only data where the question asks is an excluded source has none there under the status rule (C45, `region.availability.has_data_where_asked`): "fertility rate in Kenya excluding the World Development Indicators" answers from the Kenya census. Every read of the part admits only allowed provenances, before DFO precedence (`origin_catalogue.Catalogue.scoped`, bound around the part's resolve), and recall adds a row of each family an allowed dataset publishes of a recalled five-tuple none of whose recalled families an allowed dataset publishes (`clients.graph.with_rival_families`), so a part whose scope allows a family recall found, as a part naming the DAC tables that recalls DAC2a, or whose exclusion removes no recalled family, recalls what the unscoped question recalls. Candidacy stamps those datasets on the shape (`ShapeDraft.datasets`), and the part reads only their rows. A role-bearing shape the index carries declares a role axis the query names when one of its stamped datasets holds that role on every row it publishes of the family (`place_roles.lacks_query_axis`, `code_index.members.family_declares_roles`), so the shape of a part naming a donor-only dataset lacks the recipient though another dataset of the family holds it; a family no dataset publishes declares the axes its rows hold. A shape whose one eligible dataset is opt-in names that dataset (`ShapeDraft.named_dataset`), so a part electing it binds the source slot and the dataset's invariants as a part naming the dataset does. A part that names a source takes candidacy before the score floor, the prunes and the ranking, since naming a table may elect a family the unscoped ranking puts lower, and refuses qualifier_not_bound before any bind call when its scope leaves none of its recalled shapes. A part that only excludes sources chooses among the readings the unscoped question reaches: the score floor, the measure-class check, the prunes, the five-tuple family election and the ranking run unscoped, and candidacy then filters the ranked menu before the top-K cap (`phase_rank_bind._phase_prune_and_rank`), unless every exclusion removes nothing; when the filter removes a reading the menu would have offered without the exclusion and what it leaves seats nothing, no shape or only the leaves of roots larger than the menu, the part refuses qualifier_not_bound before any bind call. A part the prunes or a declining bind call leave with no reading it accepts refuses qualifier_not_bound naming its sources only when its scope removed a reading (`ResolveState.scope_removed_readings`, `phase_rank_bind._declined_terminal`), and refuses variable_not_resolved otherwise. A part that names a source has a reading removed at candidacy. A part that only excludes has one removed when the menu would have offered a reading without the exclusion and does not offer it with it (`seating.seat_menu` over the unexcluded list and over what the filter leaves): a reading the filter took, or one a root displaced that seats only once the filter took some of its leaves. A leaf of a root larger than the menu, or a shape past the last seat, is offered neither way and is no loss to the part, and an excluding part whose unscoped prunes leave no reading has had none removed by its scope. Either refusal names in unbound_qualifiers the sources it named, else those it excluded, then each opt-in dataset in scope that publishes a recalled shape, with no flagged_match. A word every declared label of the datasets the part answers from holds (the settled dataset's, else each one the elected shape answers from) names the table and restricts nothing. An unplaced phrase of such words records nothing, and the qualifier coverage rule (C93) reads such a word as covered. A word those datasets state of the rows the part reads (`DatasetDeclaration.stated_words`, `phase_rank_bind._dataset_stated_words`) restricts nothing on them and is read the same way, by the coverage rule, in an unplaced phrase, in the code terms of a part naming a source, and by a value requirement: a dataset states a word of the rows with one measured property, and where it names a kind of party, of the rows whose party on that flow axis is of the kind. Every CRS row states "bilateral"; a DAC2a or DAC2b `amount` row states it where the part binds its recipient to countries, and states nothing where the recipient is an organisation or the part leaves the recipient open. So "bilateral ODA to Kenya" mints the `spec_id` "ODA to Kenya" mints, and "bilateral ODA grants to Kenya" on a part naming the CRS refuses nothing. The part states a word where its variable or a typed qualifier phrase holds it as a modifier of the measure, with no function word, connective or separator before it (`bind_repair.word_stated`); a word an occurrence negates or excludes is not stated. A recipient holds the kind only where every reading it can settle to has it (`place_roles.place_set_kinds`): a pinned recipient has the kind of the entity its dcid resolved to, a group the kind of its members, and a group the recipient role admits at its own node the node's kind too, so the EU, whose node is an organisation, holds none. In the five-tuple family election a family reads a word it states beside a rival the code index carries that reads the word in no phrase, and a stated word adds nothing beside a rival that codes it or one the index does not carry (`bound_values.elect_five_tuple_family`). Recall searches a text holding a stated word a second time without it and adds the series of the datasets that state it (`interpret._detect`). A dataset also declares a word that names one of its parties (`DatasetDeclaration.party_words`): "multilateral" names `DACMultilaterals`, the DAC's total of the multilateral organisations, as the provider of a DAC2a or DAC2b `amount` row to a country. A part states the word as it states a stated word, on a flow axis no name of the part is directed to, no operation runs across and the part does not ask every member of, while a name is directed to each axis of the word's `where` (`place_roles.stated_party_words`). Such a part is read with the word first (`pipeline.resolve_variable`): recall searches the text without the word and adds the series of the datasets that declare it, and a role-bearing shape whose every dataset declares the word for its measured property binds the party on that axis, beside the places the query names, where they fill the word's `where` and the shape reads each word next to the word (`place_roles.held_party_words`, `_beside_words_read`, `bind_place_roles`). The party's set passes role admission as a named set does (`phase_intake._prune_shapes_by_admission`). The reading stands where a shape binding the party reads every phrase any other pooled shape reads, the binding shapes then being the pool (`phase_rank_bind.with_party_reading`), and the answer holds the party alone on the word's axis in every Spec (`pipeline.binds_each_party`). So "multilateral ODA to Kenya" mints the `spec_id` "ODA from multilaterals to Kenya" mints. Otherwise the reading is withdrawn (`decisions.PartyReadingWithdrawn`) and the part is resolved again without party words: its reply equals the reply of an index declaring no party word, and no event of the first reading is sent. "multilateral aid for education to Kenya", "multilateral development bank ODA to Kenya" and "multilateral loans to Kenya" reply so, as does a part naming the provider itself, which states no party word. A declared party the graph gives no type has no set, and the read is logged and noted as degraded (`phase_intake._party_word_sets`). A part naming a source refuses qualifier_not_bound before any bind call on a variable code term no row of its candidate shapes holds (`phase_rank_bind._undeclared_qualifiers`). Every no_data outcome a scoped part reaches past compile, at candidacy, election or the read, names the sources that limited it (`RegionResult.source_scope`, set in `pipeline.resolve_variable`): a qualifier_not_bound outcome lists in unbound_qualifiers its category terms, then the sources the part named, else those it excluded, then any other source it could not bind, each once (a part that names sources, none of whose tables holds its bindings on one row, names those sources alone, `phase_election._unserved_sources`), as an unbound entry under its label whose `named` holds its references; the message and rendered_sentence close on "The search read only <labels>." or "The search left out <labels>." (`render.no_data.scope_sentence`), and a qualifier_not_bound outcome naming no term reads "No series for this query comes from <labels>." or "... from a source other than <labels>.". The bind menu offers no source, and no bind-model draft binds the source. One rule names a dataset on a shape that names none (`bound_values.part_source`), applied to the elected shape at election vetting and to each alternate when it is walked: the shape names the dataset the part is served from among its stamped datasets within its scope, whenever its unnamed identity would resolve to another dataset at the Datasets mint, which selects a family by the identity's slot keys. A scope excluding the dataset that would serve so names the one the election serves among the eligible others, and a shape whose slot keys select another family than its own names its own dataset. A shape the index does not carry names none. The rule reads the role keys the identity binds its places under only where two or more datasets could serve. Under a scope leaving a shape one dataset every candidate names it. Each alternate is named by the same rule under its own representative's bindings and role keys, as the elected shape is under the model's, so an alternate's identity is the one a question electing its shape under the same scope mints. A code the bindings bind, the model's or one the query text forces, that a publisher of the family outside the scope holds and no row of the part's datasets holds refuses qualifier_not_bound naming the code (`phase_rank_bind._unheld_bound_codes`). A code term of the variable, read in every code system, that no row of the part's named dataset holds and that the elected shape's own codes do not read word for word refuses qualifier_not_bound once the bindings are final, in either coverage mode. The term is an unbound record with evidence `unavailable` naming its codes, and the elected identity is the flagged_match (`phase_rank_bind._unavailable_qualifiers`). "multilateral ODA", DAC1 aid type 2000, on a part naming the CRS refuses with CRS ODA as the near match. A part naming a source whose bind call elects no shape refuses qualifier_not_bound on the code terms no offered shape holds, with no flagged_match (`phase_rank_bind._unelected_qualifiers`): "education aid" naming IDS names CRS purpose 110 and IDS. A refusal on an exclusion settles the answer of a part that only excludes sources (`ResolveState.scope_settled`, set in `phase_rank_bind.exclusion_records` alone), so its UN pass does not run (C51e): our catalogue holds the measure and the part left its publishers out. Three refusals settle: the filter leaving no reading that seats, a declining bind call after the filter removed a reading, and the scoped read finding the elected data excluded where the question asks (`pipeline._excluded_from_the_place`). A refusal of the election for codes or rows the part's datasets lack settles nothing, whatever source it names, and nor does any refusal of a part that names a source; such a part reaches the UN pass when its scope allows a UN System Data Commons provenance. A part whose scope allows no UN System Data Commons provenance holds no UN shape and never waits for the UN resolve (C51e, `phase_intake._un_pool`); a part whose exclusions leave a UN provenance allowed keeps its UN shapes on the same terms as the undeclared tables of ours. On the raw_text door the extraction names no source; the engine scans the query against the source dictionary (`source_scan.scan_sources`): at each word the longest key that runs over whole words, ignoring case, except that a one-word key of at most five characters, an abbreviation such as IDS, CRS, WHO or UN, matches only where the query writes it in capitals. A hit inside a longer entity name is that entity ("Catholic Relief Services (CRS)"); a hit whose words are a whole variable is the measure ("adolescent birth rate"); a hit inside a longer code label the query writes is that code's words ("UN" in "UN peacekeeping"); a hit naming an organisation, a Source or a collection such as the DAC, that is an entity or lies in a variable's words is a party to the flow ("debt service to the World Bank", "World Bank core contributions"), unless a data word follows it ("OECD data") or "according to" precedes it; a hit naming a provenance or Dataset is a source wherever it stands ("education aid to Kenya from the CRS"). A source is excluded when a stretch of the query the door read as leaving something out names it (C170a), each item of the stretch's list on its own, and included otherwise: an item names a source when every word of what it leaves out, less articles, "from" and data words ("data", "figures", "database"), lies in the source's words, so "excluding CRS data" and "excluding the CRS database" leave out the CRS (`source_scan.left_out_sources`). An excluded source attaches from the stretch's blank, and a stretch the door kept unblanked excludes none. Each source qualifies the variables whose words hold its words, else the variable it directly precedes, else the nearest variable before it, else the nearest after it; one variable takes every source, and a source no rule places qualifies every variable, so "WEO inflation and population" scopes inflation to the WEO alone. A dropped qualifier that only names a scanned source ("excluding the CRS", "selon le SNPC") is applied by its mention and raises no warning (`source_scan.source_phrases`). An extracted entity whose words a scanned source covers is that source and no entity of the part, so "OECD data on education aid to Kenya" has Kenya as its only entity (`source_scan.SourceScan.source_entities`). Each part compiles its variable's mentions as a parsed part's are (`entry.raw_text._prepare_extraction`). On every door a part's variable text leaves out the words of each mention its scope resolved to a source, compared as the dictionary reads them, unless nothing would remain (`source_scope.without_source_words`, applied in `entry.parsed.interpret_parts` and `fanout._stream_one_variable`): "DAC2a ODA" naming DAC2a recalls, binds, restates and mints as "ODA" naming DAC2a. Words naming no admitted source are no mention, so raw text does not refuse source_not_resolved, and a key naming two unrelated sources refuses source_ambiguous. Words that leave a part no source answer that part qualifier_not_bound before any bind call, naming the sources it named, else those it excluded, while the other parts answer (`region.result.empty_scope_result`); parsed mentions that leave a part no source refuse request_invalid at the mention that empties the scope. The raw-text response cache keys on each variable's mentions. A mention posted with a ref reads one way. The bind model sees the codes of every dataset the part may answer from, less the slots they all pin to one value. The family's dataset is elected from the bindings and the role keys the identity binds its places under (`code_index.members.elect_dataset`): the named dataset, else among the part's datasets one whose rows cover the role keys serves before one whose rows do not, then one whose family declares exactly the roles those keys place, then the lowest rank. A slot all the elected dataset's index rows of the five-tuple hold with one value (`CodeIndex.dataset_pins`, read through `code_index.members.elected_pins`, for example `dac2aAidType` and `crsTypeOfFlow` on `attributedAmount`) is a pin: the election implies it, no declared default binds it, a qualifier naming its value is bound, and the sentence states no pinned slot. Every identity Search mints or reads is canonical (`code_index.members.canonical_pins`): of the pinned slots the identity declares bound to their pin or unbound, the largest subset whose release leaves the election and the family unchanged is unbound, the first in lexicographic order of sorted property names among subsets of one size, and every other one is bound to its pin (a code only that dataset reports, bound by the question), whichever of them the bind model or the client bound. A first answer mints its identity canonical, a refusal's flagged identity included, whatever the bind model drafted; dataset_resubmit and the options door read the posted identity in canonical form, so an identity posted with a pin bound and one posted without mint one `spec_id`, elect one family and denote one population. The resubmit and the options door refuse `facet_not_available` for an identity naming a dataset that does not publish its family, whatever slots it declares, as the Datasets mint does. The measure vote that forces open measure axes reads the same election. resolved.trace.resolved_sources and the sentence name the one dataset elected for an index-resolved family, under its declared label. A disclosed alternative candidate names the dataset its shape names, as the elected shape does, so its identity is the one a question electing it mints, and an alternative may answer from another dataset than the elected part. A candidate folded into resolved.alternative_measures as a measure sibling carries the identity a question electing its shape under the answer's bindings mints: the sibling shape's slots, the dataset that shape names, in canonical form. The parsed door and the parsed entries of a dataset_resubmit request interpret their parts alike (`parsed.interpret_parts`), so an identical part answers the same family with the same warnings on either door. A part's source words match whole against the origin catalogue's names and the registry's aliases, and no door binds a source from free text any other way | C81    | resolved.coverage is exact, breadth, or bare, all carrying has_data. Breadth carries an ordered dimensions list; a role-bearing discovered scope's own breadth carries the open axis (recipients or providers) first, then the source-coverage dimension(s) the role index contributes, then a trailing member-variables dimension counting the StatVars the scope drew from, with window set to the requested window or null; an exact coverage's evidence reads facet_metadata when its observation_count comes from the facet index and no series ships in that payload, and observed when a preview series ships beside it, including a partial delivery where some requested series failed and an observations_probe_failed warning names the read that failed; the count is the facet-index count in either case (an SSE response event always reads facet_metadata) | C83    | Each series carries stat_var{ref,label}, entities (list of {role,ref,label}), axes, facet (per-axis map), points, and total_obs. axes lists {property, ref, label}, one per constraint the series' StatVar declares with a resolved value, ordered by property dcid: property is the constraint property's dcid, ref the value's dcid, and label the value's graph label, else the value dcid's tail split into lower-case words, as the StatVar's row label names an unlabelled value. A StatVar with no constraints carries axes []. The Datasets observations door serves the same axes for a snapshot share | C170   | Each part carries `departures`, an ordered list of `{code, binding_ref, asked, shown, text}`, `[]` when the answer is what was asked. `code` is a closed `DepartureCode` value, never a warning or refusal code; the list follows its precedence (closest_match, measure_not_applied, operation_not_applied, breakdown_not_applied, entity_unavailable, member_unavailable, year_unavailable, modelled_values, group_read_as_members, whole_total_read, whole_total_unpublished, direction_discarded, direction_inferred, scope_limited) and the model refuses any other order. `binding_ref` names the identity binding whose edit clears the departure, null when no binding edit clears it; a client links the departure to the token carrying the same `binding_ref` when the plan has one, and a candidates or no_data plan, or a how slot the heading leaves silent, may have none. `text` is one or two short sentences for people, non-empty; on a no_data part no text claims the part shows anything. A `closest_match` departure tells a client to draw a muted "Closest match:" lead before the heading, a lead no spec token carries. `retrieval_score_weak` alone writes no departure. A Spec whose one group leaves out a member holding data, as a mint of it names that member in `coverage_below_scope`'s `unserved_members`, carries one `member_unavailable` departure: `asked` names the members left out, `shown` the source the answer reads, and `binding_ref` is `source:` where the answer binds a source slot, `how:<property dcid>` where a set binding names the members, and null otherwise; its text says the source read holds no values for the members under the unit and method read, names that source and its unit where the reading holds them, so each reading of a candidates part states its own, and says nothing of why they were read. A candidates part carries one for each reading that leaves a member out. A Spec whose group serves every member holding data, or leaves out only members holding none, carries none. A candidates part whose readings were elected in place of a measure that holds no data, its `elected_shape_no_data` warning on the part, carries one `closest_match` departure, whose text speaks of the readings and whose `shown` is null, unless one reading names the measure asked by the test a definite part's reading takes (a content word of `variable_text` in its measure noun, in every one of its StatVar names, or in a name of a code it binds), as "GDP" answered with Kenya's GDP and its GDP per capita; a client draws the lead once, before the first reading. A part showing one member of a family whose total holds no data carries `closest_match` beside its `family_total_absent` warning, which holds that Spec's `spec_id`, with `shown` the member's measure noun; a candidates part leaves it out. A resubmit, whose request names no measure in words, raises it with `asked` null, and its text states only the measure shown ("This shows neonatal mortality rate, the closest measure the data holds."). A no_data part whose sentence shows an alternate reading (gate `only_alternate_has_data`) carries that reading's `closest_match` departure when the rule fires, and no other loose-match wording. A no_data part answering `no_observations` for years its elected series holds no point in carries `year_unavailable` with `shown` null, naming the years asked and the span the series runs, unless its sentence shows such an alternate. An `entity_unavailable` departure carries its role's token ref and `asked` naming the entities the request named that hold no values in the series the answer reads; for a role the request named as a group, `asked` is the request's own words for the group and the text counts the members of the group as the heading names it, naming none of them. A part states one `entity_unavailable` per role, naming every entity on the role the data holds no values for: those its read plan serves no point at, including entities whose rows a conversion into each entity's own currency left out (`currency_unmapped_omitted`); the places the UN front door refused (`un_system_unavailable`) when every StatVar the part reads is a UN one; and on a no_data part warned `withheld_by_registry` the places it names, its text then naming the sources the origin registry withholds that hold values there. A `modelled_values` departure states the served method when it is modelled or estimated, and fires with only some values modelled when a served series selects such a method or holds points converted on a projected exchange rate or deflator (`deflator_projected`). A `group_read_as_members` departure's `asked` is the request's own words for the group ("EU institutions"), the group's label when the request named it by no other words. On a flow role, its text also names the residuals the group's declared total counts beside the members and a dataset the Spec serves publishes (`PartyRow.residuals`, `PartyRow.datasets`), as "and leaves out what the data allocates to no single country: Africa, unallocated by country (DAC); Eastern Africa, unallocated by country (DAC)", each label once. For a group whose declared total is not its members' sum (`PartyRow.sums_members` false), the text names no residual and, when a dataset the Spec serves publishes that total on the role, adds "and the source publishes its own figure for the group, which can differ from one built from its members". A group read as its total writes no `group_read_as_members` departure. A resubmit's request holds no words, so every departure on it has `asked` null, `entity_unavailable` on a group or a named entity included. A text that quotes `asked` on another door states there only what the answer shows or left out: the members shown, as the heading names them ("This shows the 27 European Union members, each on its own."), the readings ("These readings are the closest measures the data holds."), and the span the data covers ("The data ends in 2024, so this shows 1980 to 2024."). Other texts stand. A `forecast` operation to a target year departs as `year_unavailable` when that year falls outside the served span, and as `operation_not_applied` otherwise. A phrase extraction dropped writes a `measure_not_applied` departure on the unit token when it names a price basis, and on no token when it holds a content word and no operation word ("Trend in", "compare the" write none). A stretch the raw_text door read as leaving something out writes a `measure_not_applied` departure on no token unless every answer leaves it out, whatever the heading states (C170a). A qualifier whose content words one heading and byline the part shows already holds, source included, writes none, on a candidates part as on a definite one; and a price basis writes one only with `shown`, the byline's unit text. `direction_discarded` fires only when the request's own words gave the place its direction (a `from` or `to` before it, or a parsed role text). `direction_inferred` fires when the request named a place without a direction and only one direction holds data there, so the answer reads the place in that direction; its text names the place and the role it reads. Bound values that together match no member of the elected measure are unbound and the measure read again: values the engine chose (a declared default, the elected measure partition) go first and depart nothing, then the request's own values go. A request-named or posted value is a code pinned on a property the shape holds (C189), so on every door a definite read without it refuses `qualifier_not_bound`, naming the value once as an unbound term with the read as flagged_match and no departure beside it; a candidates part carries its `measure_not_applied` departure on its how token instead (`binding_ref` `how:<property>`, `asked` the request's words for the value), except for a value the bind model drafted on a property the elected shape lacks, which every candidate applies (C189) and none departs. A set drafted on such a property is met by membership, and each value of it that neither an answer's bindings nor any member it reads holds departs `measure_not_applied` with `query_qualifier_dropped` once, on a definite part as on a candidates part, beside no refusal. An alternate reading's values come from its representative StatVar, so it unbinds them silently. A resubmit's values are all ones its request posted, so every one it unbinds refuses the same way, as does a single edited value no elected member carries. A no_data part whose check covered values the engine chose carries one `scope_limited` departure, its `binding_ref` the how token of the first such value and its text naming only what the check covered ("This check covered only ODA."), and its plan's departure tokens equal its `departures` texts | C170a  | On the raw_text door one small model call beside extraction reads each stretch of the query that asks to leave something out (`exclusion_read.read_exclusions`), the question alone and in any language, as `{phrase, left_out}`: the stretch verbatim and the words naming what it leaves out. A measure defined by absence ("share of the population without access to electricity") is no such stretch, since deleting it changes the measure, and neither are a category name ("non-DAC countries") or a limit ("no later than 2015"). The phrase is found in the query as layer 1 keys it (`response_cache_keys.normalise_query`: NFKC, whitespace collapsed) and blanked at the query's own characters, or in the query's NFC text where NFKC cannot be mapped back to them. A stretch is admitted (`exclusion_read.admit_exclusions`) when it occurs there as whole words and is shorter than the query, no occurrence lies inside a run the query uses as one code, measure or party label (`qualifier_match.label_runs`: "official development assistance excluding debt relief" is DAC2a aid type 250), and something outlives the phrases stating the part's provider scope (`stated_scope.scope_phrase_spans`) and the noun each runs into before a comma or coordinator ("not including multilateral flows" is that scope alone). Only what outlives them is blanked, with ";": "ODA to Kenya not including multilateral or humanitarian aid" reads "ODA to Kenya not including multilateral ;", whose scope is still bilateral. A read phrase gives one stretch per negation cue, and the items under one cue share it (`exclusion_read._pieces`): an item opens a stretch when it starts the phrase, when words of its own come before the first word it leaves out, or when an applied scope phrase stands before it, which no stretch covers. Each stretch carries, in `normalise_query` form, the span the answer quotes (`LeftOut.phrase`), the words its blank removed, which alone are judged (`LeftOut.shown`), and the items it leaves out (`LeftOut.things`). A stretch is quoted from its cue, and an item after a scope phrase with no cue of its own as the words its blank removed, its coordinator included: that query's stretch quotes "or humanitarian aid" and judges "humanitarian aid", "not including multilateral and without IMF credit" quotes and judges "without IMF credit", and "excluding debt relief and not including multilateral or loans" gives "excluding debt relief" and "or loans". A stretch the query does not write as read is kept unblanked and disclosed. A query with nothing blanked reaches extraction byte-identical. The blanked text is the only text extraction parses and every reader of the query's words past the door reads, so a word left out reaches no field; only `query_echo` and the layer 1 key take the query as written. The read and a speculative extraction of the query start together and the read is awaited first: on a hit the speculative parse is cancelled when the read is admitted and the blanked text is parsed, the discarded parse's tokens counted in `diagnostics.llm_usage` when it had finished and one `uncounted_calls`, a call sent whose tokens no response reported, when it had not (`entry.raw_text._discarded_usage`). The read sits behind the extraction store under its own key, so a repeated query spends neither call, and it reports as the `exclusion_read` step of `diagnostics.timing_ms.by_step`. A failed read raises `LLMInfraError` as a failed extraction does and fails the request with a retryable 503 `service_unavailable`: an answer served without the read could include what the query asked to leave out, and say nothing. A blanked text that parses to no variable refuses `variable_not_resolved`, and one that parses to a catalogue, out-of-scope or requires-analysis question keeps that disposition; either carries a `query_qualifier_dropped` warning for each stretch that leaves out more than sources. A stretch is read on its own for sources: an item of its list whose left-out words a source covers leaves that source out as an excluded mention (`source_scan.scan_sources`, C78b), and a stretch leaving out sources alone raises nothing further. Search does not compute an exclusion: "ODA to Africa except Kenya" answers Africa and says so. Each other stretch qualifies every measure, but the measures of one clause when its blank lies in it (`source_scan._stretch_attach`, `SourceScan.stretch_targets`): each measure takes the first span of the query no longer measure took (`source_scan._measure_positions`), measures written joined only by commas and coordinators share a clause, which runs to the next clause's first measure, and a ";" between two measures parts them, whether a blank or written in the query, so "ODA and OOF to Kenya excluding debt relief" judges both, "ODA excluding debt relief and OOF to Kenya" judges ODA alone, and "ODA to Kenya and GDP of Kenya excluding humanitarian aid" judges GDP alone. A stretch before every measure, with no blank, or in a query that does not write each measure qualifies every measure. It is judged on those parts alone. On a part whose answer does not leave it out it carries a `query_qualifier_dropped` warning quoting it and a `measure_not_applied` departure whose text is `You asked for "<phrase>". The search did not apply it.`, and the envelope carries the warning while one such part stands. An answer leaves a stretch out when it binds the value binding repair bound for the stretch's own label on an open slot (`bind_repair.apply_named_exclusions`: "without IMF credit" binds `includesIMFCredit=WithoutIMFCredit`, and a label whose every word lies in the items the stretch leaves out binds nothing), judged on each final answer and dropped when the value is unbound (`ResolveState.require_named_exclusion`, `forget_bound`), or when every word of what it leaves out lies in runs of its judged words naming codes of a partitioning property, a measure axis or a code system declared `summable`, and the answer meets every property each run names (`state.exclusion_requirements`, `requirements.Judgement.met_exclusions`). On a constraint property it binds values outside the run's codes ("gross ODA, not net" answers gross and states nothing); on a measure axis the shape carries (`populationType`, `measuredProperty`, `measurementQualifier`) it does not read the run's value (`qualifier_readings.answer_reads_measure`), so "GDP of Kenya not in constant prices" answered in current prices states nothing. A property the answer's shape lacks, and a measure axis on which it states no value, is summed over, so a run naming one is not left out, unless a unit request the answer is served under states its price basis. The requirements are read once per request, each phrase once. Layer 2 keys on each stretch with the words it judges, what it leaves out and the parts it qualifies. The parsed door has no stretch: its parts state what they ask for. | implemented | Pinned by `tests/engine/test_exclusion_read.py`, `tests/engine/test_source_scan.py::test_a_stretch_is_read_for_the_sources_it_leaves_out`, `tests/engine/test_bind_repair.py::TestApplyNamedExclusions` and `tests/engine/test_response_cache_keys.py::test_what_a_query_leaves_out_changes_layer2_key`. |

| C197   | Each part carries `assumptions`, an ordered list of `{kind, binding_ref, text, refs}` naming every value the engine filled that the request did not ask for, `[]` when it filled none. `kind` is a closed `AssumptionKind` value: `name_corrected`, on the raw-text door, an entity the Spec binds whose extracted name replaced query words (`ExtractedEntity.span`) that list no name or alias of it ("Germny" for Germany): the span and the name resolve in one resolver call, and the name corrects the span when it settles on one entity the span's own resolution does not name (`surface_resolution.corrected_surfaces`), so a listed alias ("Kenia" for Kenya), a choice the name settles ("Trinidad" for Trinidad and Tobago), a translation with a null span, and a national adjective whose role cue is its own span ("French aid") are none, `binding_ref` the entity's role token ref, `text` the quoted words and the Spec's label ('"Germny" read as Germany'), left out when that passes the 200-character text bound, `refs` the entity dcid, `asked` the typed words ("Germny"), a field no other kind carries; `declared_default`, a how slot whose trace records `declared_default` provenance, `binding_ref` `how:<property dcid>`, `text` the bound code's full label in the code index ("official development assistance" for `CRSTypeOfFlow10`, whose short name is "ODA"), or for a value the index holds no row for its graph label without the property suffix, `refs` the slot's bound value dcids; a part a same-shape merge serves reads that provenance from its own request's Spec and states the values its slot fixed there that the merged slot still binds; `open_role`, a flow role no request word asked for: a role the role scope default filled (`resolved.scope` on that axis) whatever its member count, or a role the heading states with no entity of its own (a count, or "all creditors"; the destination shares the where slot, so a place that slot binds with no direction of its own names the destination), on an axis the identity neither binds in `entities` nor lists in `open_roles`, that none of the part's operations runs over (an `over` of `origin` or `destination`, as "top 5 donors" ranks over the providers; an `over` of `place` asks no flow axis), and that the request's words did not ask every member of ("all donors"), the operation axes and the identity's open roles read through one rule (`search.models.asked_open_axes`), which also opens those axes for resolution, `binding_ref` the role's token ref (`origin:` or `where:`), `text` "every" and the role's singular noun in the answer's flow family ("every provider", "every creditor"); `place_scope`, a `resolved.scope` on `observationAbout`, `binding_ref` `where:`, `text` "the world as a whole" for Earth alone, "every country" when every member's `entity_type` is `Country`, else "every place"; the place scope is the where slot's one item, so the destination, which shares that slot, states no `open_role` beside it. The order is name corrections in `resolved.entities` order, declared defaults in `trace.slot_filters` order, open roles in the heading's role order, then the place scope. A measure election, the price basis and an open year are no assumptions. A definite part reads its list off its Spec, a candidates part carries the items every list in its `reading_assumptions` holds alike, compared on the whole item, `text` included, in the first reading's order, and no_data and unsupported parts carry `[]`. A candidates part's `reading_assumptions` maps each shown reading's `spec_id` to the list a definite part answering that Spec for the same request carries, read with the part's operations and every-member axes; a client that picks a reading posts its identity as a `dataset_resubmit` identity entry echoing `reading_assumptions[spec_id]`, and the rebuilt part states that list while it holds. The rules run on every door. `refs` is `[]` on `open_role` and `place_scope`, the entity dcid on `name_corrected`. A `dataset_resubmit` identity entry records its posted slots as `binder`, and an axis its measure family election forces as `measure_elected`. When it carries the optional `assumptions` echo, the part states the echoed items that still hold, with `text` read from the rebuilt Spec: a `declared_default` whose slot (`binding_ref`) still binds the echoed `refs`, an `open_role` the rebuilt answer still reads open on that axis, a `place_scope` the rebuilt answer still yields, and a `name_corrected` whose entity (`refs`) the rebuilt Spec still binds on the echoed role and that carries `asked`, restated from those words and the rebuilt Spec's label, whatever the echoed `text` says. An item the echo lacks is not stated, so an edit of the defaulted slot to another value drops it. The rebuilt trace keeps the posted provenance. An empty echo states none. An entry without `assumptions` states the open roles and place scope the rebuilt answer reads and no declared default, since the posted identity binds every slot. A `dataset_resubmit` candidates entry's `ResubmitCandidate` carries the same optional `assumptions` echo, its reading's `reading_assumptions[spec_id]`, with the same bounds and matching rule: the rebuilt part's `reading_assumptions` entry for that reading states the echoed items that still hold, and its `assumptions` are the items every such list holds alike. A candidate without `assumptions` states the open roles and place scope its rebuilt reading reads. Candidates that rebuild to one `spec_id` keep the first in posted order, with its own echo, as a fresh candidate set keeps one reading per `spec_id`. A set that keeps two or more readings drops a repeat with no warning. When the candidates that resolve rebuild to one `spec_id`, the part is that reading's definite part stating the first such candidate's echo, and its `candidates_collapsed_definite` warning says how many still resolve and, when more than one does, that they rebuild to one reading. The sentence carrying the part closes with them as one clause of `segment="assumption"` text tokens, after its departures: " ", "Assumed: ", the texts with "; " between, ".". A joined envelope sentence writes one clause naming each part's items in part order, each item under its part's `part_index`, an item two parts share under none, and the clause's leading space, "Assumed: ", separators and "." under the one part that owns every item, else under none. A part others fold into states the items of every part folded into it as well. An item every one of them carries has no `part_index`, an item one part alone carries has that part's, whether it is the part folded into or a folded one, and an item several but not all carry has none. A sentence written part by part closes each part's sentence with its own clause. A part whose sentence and clause both repeat an earlier part's is stated once with it, that sentence and clause under no `part_index`, followed by each part's own departures, and a part whose clause differs keeps its own sentence. | C172   | A role whose entities the request named as a group carries the group on the Spec's `resolved.entity_groups[role]` as `{ref, label, entity_type, total, group_id}`. `ref` is the group's graph node and `entity_type` that node's graph type (`Continent`, `UNGeoRegion`, `ContinentalUnion`), as each entry of `resolved.entities` carries its own. Both are null when the graph holds no node for the group. Such a group, read from the membership registry ("Asia (DAC)"), names the registry's id for it in `group_id` (`groups/OECD.DAC.Region.Asia`), and `group_id` is null whenever `ref` is set, so a group carries one id or neither. With a `ref`, `label` is the graph's label for that node on a first answer and on a resubmit, which ignores the posted `label` and the posted `entity_type` and reads both from the graph; without one, or where the graph gives no label, `label` is the request's own name for the group. On a `dataset_resubmit`, a client posts a Spec's `resolved.entity_groups` as `entity_groups` beside the `identity` it posts, on an identity entry and on each candidate. A resubmit keeps a posted group only when it posts a `ref` and the graph's members of that node are the role's entities, and otherwise drops it so the role reads as its members. The members of a continent or subregion that the membership policy holds or omits (`MembershipPolicy.holds_region`) are the countries the policy holds for it, which a first answer binds (`expand_places`), whatever its graph type, and those of any other node its `<-containedInPlace` subjects, so a first answer that bound a region's members resubmits to the same `spec_id` with its group. A posted group with no `ref` and a `group_id` is kept when the registry's members of that id, under the default projection, are the role's entities, with the total the code index declares for that id (`CodeIndex.totals_by_group`) kept aside, so the resubmit answers the same `spec_id` with the same group and the same `group_read_as_members` departure; a posted `group_id` the registry does not hold, or one a graph node retired (`groups/G7`), answers `request_invalid` at `/input/entity_groups/<role>/group_id`. Any other posted group with no `ref` drops, since nothing ties its label to the role's entities, and so does a group on a role the identity leaves unbound. A departure naming the group (`group_read_as_members`, `entity_unavailable`) asks for it in the request's own words. A group the code index declares a flow dataset's total for (`CodeIndex.totals_by_group`: Africa's `DACAfrica`, the G7's `GroupOfSeven`) binds that total party on a flow role in place of its members (resolvekit's "Africa (DAC)" and the other DAC regions whose members are a graph place's take that place's id, so "Africa (DAC)" reaches `DACAfrica`) when the Spec's own read holds data there and the request did not ask for the members, and the role then carries the group with `total` naming that party; any other role carries `total` null. A role carrying a `total` also carries `members`, the members of the total's group as the membership registry lists them under its default projection, sorted (the 32 DAC member countries for `DACCountriesTotal`), so a caller can tell whether a party sits inside the total; `members` is absent where the role binds the members or the registry lists none for the group, and a resubmit derives it whatever it posts. A resubmit keeps a posted group with a `total` only when the identity binds exactly that party on the role and the code index declares it the total of the posted `ref`'s group, or of the posted `group_id`'s group, or of any group when the group posts neither. `definition.identity` carries no group and is hashed whole. A client that hashes it with RFC 8785 and SHA-256 gets `spec_id`. An identity posted with `entity_groups` inside it is refused. Search answers `request_invalid`, and the Datasets mint and register doors answer `request_invalid` too, with the kit's `unknown_member` in the detail text. `resolved.entity_groups` exposes the groups the spec still binds, and the heading names that role as the group with its member count ("the 27 EU member states"). A group whose `ref` is a continent or subregion that the membership policy holds or omits (`MembershipPolicy.holds_region`) reads as its countries ("the 54 countries in Africa"), whatever its graph type, in the heading and in a departure that counts the group. `entity_type` is informational, and the wording keys on `ref`. A flow role read as the whole population of its side carries `whole_of`, the classes of party that population covers (`official`, `private`, `residual`), and `whole_of` is absent on every other group: a DAC2a or DAC2b part that names no donor, names "official donors", or sums over no axis binds `DACOfficialDonors` (`total` naming it, `whole_of` `["official"]`), a DAC2a part asking 1984 to 2024 with no recipient named binds `DACAllRecipients` (`["official", "residual"]`), and "all donors", "every donor" and "all donors including private", phrases of one whole total set, bind `DACOfficialDonors` and `DACPrivateDonors` on DAC2a (`label` "all donors including private", `total` null, `whole_of` `["official", "private"]`). A total on a side the part totals binds only where the request's coverage snapshot holds a series at the total of the variables the part's final bindings elect, those settled with the total bound, the measure election's forced values and the dataset's canonical pins included. Those totals are tried in a fixed order, each settled on its own, and the first that holds binds: the donors' before the recipients', so a part naming neither side binds `DACAllRecipients` where `DACOfficialDonors` holds no series, and once a whole population a text names binds on one side, no total binds on the other; where none holds, each side keeps its members, and the part answers as a request offered no total does. An alternate, and the other direction of a place named without one, judges its own total under its own bindings. A side the part opens, or any operation runs over (a sum, count, rank, share or report), keeps its members: an operation over a side may filter its parties ("donors giving more than 100 million"), so "from all donors" with `open_roles: ["donor"]` lists the donors. A published total stands in for a side only for an unconditional sum over its whole population: a side no text names and no operation runs over, read alone or under a sum over no axis. A whole population posted with `members: true`, or named on a side an operation runs over ("sum aid from official donors giving more than 100 million"), asks for every party of its side: no total binds, and the part answers as `open_roles` naming that side does, warning `query_qualifier_dropped` where a named place fills the side, on a shape whose datasets hold on that side, totals aside, only parties of classes the population's markers cover. An open side lists every party its dataset holds, so every other shape is pruned: "official donors" lists on ONE Imputed Multilateral Sectors and on no CRS or DAC2a shape, which hold private donors, and "all donors" lists on the CRS and not on DAC2a, which also holds a residual donor the population leaves out. The side it opens was named, so it warns no `role_scope_defaulted`. With no shape left, the part refuses `entity_role_not_admitted`. Its text names each population no shape lists exactly, with the classes of party every one of those datasets holds beside it, and the total or named members as the ways to ask instead; where each population lists exactly on some shape and no shape lists them all, it names those some shape fails as populations no source lists together. A population every shape lists exactly is never named. A whole population whose stated direction is the other side ("from France to official donors") refuses `role_incompatible`, whether it is named, posted with `members: true`, or under an operation over a side, before any shape is derived, so no listing or variable refusal stands in front of it; the refusal names each such text in `no_data.entity_terms` and says it belongs on the other side. A whole population a text names ("all donors", "to all recipients", "France and official donors") binds its parties as named parties on any shape declaring its side, so role admission, the bind menu, availability and binding read it as they read any named party. Reading it whole is a label on those parties: the side carries `whole_of` where the population stands alone on it and every dataset of the shape marks it over the requested years, and otherwise binds the same parties with no `whole_of` ("all recipients" in 1983). Where no series holds it, the part departs `entity_unavailable` for it or refuses `no_observations`, as for any named party, and never answers the side's members in its place. On a side no place is named on, where no dataset of the shape marks any of the population's parties, the side reads as unnamed only where those datasets hold some party there and every one, totals aside, is of a class the population's markers cover: "all donors" (`official` and `private`) and "all recipients" (`official` and `residual`) on the CRS, whose donors and recipients are of those classes, and "official donors" on DAC1, whose donors are all official. The answer then covers every party the dataset holds on the side and departs `whole_total_unpublished`, whose `asked` is the request's words and whose text names the dataset ("You asked for all donors. OECD Creditor Reporting System (CRS) publishes no total of every donor, so this covers every donor it holds with data, private donors among them."), and warns no `role_scope_defaulted` saying nothing was named there. A candidate set states one such departure for each dataset its shown readings read the side so from, and a no-data answer states it for the alternate it shows in its place (`only_alternate_has_data`), whose text names that match ("so the closest match covers every donor it holds with data"). Beside a place named on the side ("France and all donors"), or beside a population that does not cover it ("official donors and all donors" on the CRS), the population binds as named parties, and departs `entity_unavailable` where no series holds them. "Official donors" names `DACOfficialDonors`, whose markers cover `official` alone, and the CRS holds private donors too, so on the CRS it binds as a party the CRS holds no series of: DAC2a answers where the menu offers it, and a part naming the CRS refuses `no_observations`. A resubmit derives `whole_of` from the identity's dataset and the code index, whatever group it posts on that role. A role read as its whole population also carries `side_classes`, the classes of party, totals aside, that the answer's dataset publishes on that side, sorted (`["official", "private", "residual"]` for donors in DAC2a, `["official", "residual"]` in DAC2b); a class in `side_classes` and absent from `whole_of` is outside the total, and a resubmit derives the field from the identity whatever it posts. A multilateral organisation is an official party the graph types `Organization` (`search.engine.multilaterals`): IDA and the EU institutions are, a country or a private foundation is not. An origin entry of `resolved.entities` that is one carries `multilateral: true`, and every other entry omits the field. An origin group that binds a total or reads as its whole population carries `holds_multilaterals`: for a total, whether one of its `members` is a multilateral (`false` for `DACCountriesTotal`, whose members are countries); for a whole population, whether it covers `official` parties and the answer's datasets publish a multilateral donor (`true` for `DACOfficialDonors` on DAC2a). Any other group omits it, a total whose members the registry does not list among them, and a resubmit derives it whatever it posts. The origin entry of `resolved.role_axis_facts` carries `holds_multilaterals` too: whether a member of the open population is a multilateral where the members are known, else whether the served datasets publish one as a donor, so an open CRS donor side states `true`; the destination entry omits it. A side read whole departs as `whole_total_read`, whose `asked` is the request's words for the population ("all donors"), null when the request named nothing on the side; its text says what the total holds ("Private donors are outside it." for an official donors' total where the answer's dataset holds private donors on that side, so on DAC2a and not on DAC2b; "which includes aid not allocated to a country") and how to list the members with `open_roles`. A Spec that lists a side member by member, opened by `open_roles` or defaulted, where the dataset elected for its identity publishes that side's whole total over the requested years and the coverage snapshot holds a series of the Spec's own variables at that total beside the places its other side binds, carries the info warning `whole_total_available` with `params` `{total, total_label, whole_of}`, `whole_of` comma-joined, stamped with that Spec's `spec_id`, on a first answer and a resubmit alike: a definite part's Spec, each candidate a candidate set shows, so the set states it for the readings it holds for alone, and a no-data part's flagged and alternate matches; none when the other side reads whole | C176   | `resolved.unit_conversion` states what a `facets.unit` request can convert the answer to, as `{currencies, default_base_year, role_currencies}`. `currencies` lists each currency that converts at least one served year, sorted by code, as `{code, noun, current_years, constant_years, base_year_min, base_year_max}`: the ISO 4217 code a request names; the plural noun the byline's unit token names it by (`unit_words.currency_nouns`); the longest run of consecutive served years a current-price and a constant-price request convert, each null when no year converts; and the first and last base years a constant-price request converts at. Every such year and base year is one the conversion accepts (`materialise.convert.row_terms`): the rates of the native currencies of the facets the read plan serves, whatever unit request the answer carries, and the target's rates, which a missing rate refuses for every row; and at constant prices the deflator, after the recipe's fallback entity, of at least one entity the plan serves on the recipe's deflator role, bound or open, in the row's year and in the base year. A missing deflator leaves out only its entity's rows, in that year or, for the base year, in every year, and the answer carries a `deflator_missing_omitted` warning and a `year_unavailable` departure on the unit token naming those entities and years (at most three by name, then a count that includes any entity the conversion catalogue holds no name for). When the loaded tables cannot say whose rows a constant-price conversion leaves out, the answer carries the same warning and a departure that names no entity. `default_base_year` is the `deflatorBaseYear` every table declares, and `role_currencies` maps each role bound to one entity to that entity's national currency (`nationalCurrencyOf`) among `currencies`. It is null when the served members' unit is no currency and no unit request converted them, no recipe converts them (a stock, a series not in nominal prices, a provenance that declares none), the tables convert no served year, or no conversion catalogue is loaded. Every fact comes from a catalogue the app loads at startup and reloads when the graph generation changes, so an answer makes no read for it. Startup waits at most 15 s for the first load (`_CONVERSION_CATALOGUE_STARTUP_WAIT_S`) and then serves while the load goes on in the background. A failed load logs an ERROR and is retried after 5, 15 and 60 s and then every 300 s, or at once when a reload is requested. A reload requested during a load runs once that load ends, so loads run one at a time and the last one requested publishes last. While no catalogue is loaded, a part whose answer is in a currency, or converted by a unit request, carries the `unit_conversion_unavailable` warning (severity degraded), so a null offer reads apart from an answer that cannot convert Option ids are `<axis>:<value>` on every part. `search.scope.compile` derives each default after the explicit and carried entries, never moves an explicit or carried entry, and compiles a selection the base already holds to the base and its spec_id.
